<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct an explicit integral complex affine fourfold <i>X</i> with $`X\times\mathbb A^1\cong\mathbb A^5`$ but $`X\not\cong\mathbb A^4`$. This gives a negative answer to Zariski's affine-space cancellation problem over ℂ in dimension four. The same construction disproves the Stable Coordinate Conjecture in ambient dimension five and yields smooth 𝔸<sup>3</sup>-fibrations over 𝔸<sup>1</sup> and 𝔸<sup>2</sup> that are not Zariski-locally trivial. These fibrations disprove the Dolgachev–Weisfeiler affine-fibration conjecture over these bases.

- **C1** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): The algebra $A$ in <a href="#eq:example" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:example">[eq:example]</a> is an integral complex algebra of dimension four. For an independent variable $w$, $$A[w]\simeq\mathbb C^{[5]}, \qquad A\not\simeq\mathbb C^{[4]}.$$ Consequently the Zariski cancellation problem over the complex numbers has a negative answer in dimension four.
  - Explanation: The specified integral complex fourfold has a polynomial cylinder but is not affine space.
- **C2** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-stable-coordinate)): The polynomial $H$ in <a href="#eq:example" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:example">[eq:example]</a> is not a coordinate of $P=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J]$, but it is a coordinate of $P[w]$ for one independent variable $w$. Thus the Stable Coordinate Conjecture over $\mathbb C$ fails in ambient dimension five after one stabilization.
  - Explanation: The specified polynomial becomes a coordinate after stabilization although it is not an ambient coordinate.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-con-domain)): The algebra $A$ is an integral domain of dimension four, $p\ne0$ in $A$, and $$A[p^{-1}]=\mathbb C[p,p^{-1},x,y,z]$$ is a Laurent polynomial ring in the displayed generators.
  - Explanation: Localization identifies the fraction field and supplies integrality and dimension.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-stabilization)): For the algebra $A$ in [con:setup](#con:setup), there is an algebraic $\mathbb C$-isomorphism $$A[w]\cong\mathbb C^{[5]}.$$
  - Explanation: An exponential shift and polynomial inverse give explicit coordinates on the cylinder.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-deg-valuation)): The map $S\to A$ is injective, and $S[p^{-1}]=A[p^{-1}]$. The ring $R$ is a smooth domain. On the common fraction field of $S$ and $A$ there is a discrete valuation $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p$, normalized by $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(p)=1$, coming from $S_{pS}$. For $b\in S$ and every integer $i\geq 0$, $$\label{deg:global-divisibility} \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(b)\geq i \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad b\in p^iS.$$ In particular, each nonzero $h\in S[p^{-1}]$ has a unique expression $h=p^m b$ with $m=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)\in\mathbb Z$ and $b\in S\setminus pS$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary algebra supplies a discrete valuation whose divisibility test holds globally.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-graded)): Under [deg:graded-injection](#deg:graded-injection), the associated graded algebra is the following specific subalgebra of $R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$: $$\label{deg:G} G:=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A =R[\tau,I/\tau^2] =R[\tau,f/\tau^2,g/\tau^2].$$ It is a smooth domain. Give $P=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J]$ the presentation weights $$\label{deg:presentation-weights} (\omega(p),\omega(s),\omega(u),\omega(F),\omega(J)) =(-1,0,0,2,2).$$ Then every nonzero $h\in A$ has a polynomial representative whose maximum weight is exactly $\deg h$, and there is a graded isomorphism $$\label{deg:graded-presentation} G\cong P/(H_{\mathrm{top}}),\qquad H_{\mathrm{top}}=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2, \quad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F.$$ The images of $p,s,u,F,J$ in this isomorphism are respectively $\tau,s_R,u,f/\tau^2,g/\tau^2$.
  - Explanation: Weight reduction proves the exact graded presentation over its specified coefficient algebra.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-induced-lnd)): Every nonzero LND $D$ of $A$ induces a nonzero homogeneous LND $D_0$ of $G$. If $0\ne h\in\ker D$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{in}}(h)\in\ker D_0$.
  - Explanation: The exact filtration transfers locally nilpotent derivations and their invariant initial forms.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-invariant)): If $A\cong\mathbb C^{[4]}$, then $G$ admits a nonzero homogeneous LND with a nonzero homogeneous invariant of strictly positive valuation degree.
  - Explanation: Polynomial coordinates would force a homogeneous additive action with a positive valuation degree invariant.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-principalization)): The morphism $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\widetilde R\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}R$ is the complement of the zero section in a line bundle, with fiber scaling specified by the weights $$\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(a)=\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(d)=1,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(b)=\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(c)=-1,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(u)=0.$$ The homomorphism $R\to\widetilde R$ is faithfully flat, and $I\widetilde R=(v)$. Moreover, base change gives a faithfully flat inclusion $G\hookrightarrow\widetilde G$, where $$\label{bun:pullback-algebra} \widetilde G:=G\otimes_R\widetilde R =\widetilde R[\tau,v/\tau^2]\subset\widetilde R[\tau,\tau^{-1}],\qquad \widetilde G\cong\widetilde R[\tau,V]/(\tau^2V-v).$$ Thus $V$ denotes the element $v/\tau^2$ in the displayed subalgebra. The ring $\widetilde G$ is a domain and has the following commuting gradings:* <div class="center"> \| \| *$a,d$* \| *$b,c$* \| *$u$* \| *$\tau$* \| *$V$* \| \|:------------------:\|--------:\|--------:\|------:\|---------:\|------:\| \| *Valuation degree* \| *$0$* \| *$0$* \| *$0$* \| *$-1$* \| *$2$* \| \| *Fiber weight* \| *$1$* \| *$-1$* \| *$0$* \| *$0$* \| *$2$* \| </div>
  - Explanation: A punctured line bundle principalizes the center ideal and gives a faithfully flat pullback with separate gradings.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-linearization)): Let $X$ be a smooth integral affine variety over $\mathbb C$, and let $\pi\colon\mathbb A^1\times X\to X$ be the projection. Then $$\pi^*\colon\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(\mathbb A^1\times X).$$ For every algebraic additive action $\alpha\colon\mathbb G_a\times X\to X$ and every line bundle $L$ on $X$, there is a unique isomorphism of line bundles $$\label{bun:linearization-map} \phi\colon\pi^*L\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\alpha^*L$$ whose restriction to $\{0\}\times X$ is $\mathrm{id}_L$. This isomorphism obeys the action cocycle. It therefore defines a unique action on $L$ that lifts $\alpha$, is linear on its fibers, and acts as the identity at the group identity. The action preserves $L^\times$ and commutes with fiber scaling. If $L^\times$ is affine, the induced derivation of its coordinate ring is locally nilpotent and preserves the fiber weights.
  - Explanation: Normalized fiber-linear additive lifts exist uniquely on line bundles over a smooth integral affine base.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-homogeneous-lift)): Let $D_0$ be a homogeneous locally nilpotent derivation of $G$ of valuation degree shift $j$. There is a locally nilpotent derivation $\widetilde D$ of $\widetilde G$ restricting to $D_0$ on $G$, with fiber weight zero and valuation degree shift $j$. If $D_0\ne0$, then $\widetilde D\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The normalized lift preserves the valuation shift and has fiber weight zero without requiring coefficient preservation.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-lnd-order)): Let $T$ be a domain over $\mathbb C$ and let $\delta$ be a locally nilpotent derivation of $T$. For $0\ne h\in T$, define $$\nu_\delta(h)=\max\{n\ge0:\delta^n(h)\ne0\}.$$ Then for nonzero $f,g\in T$, $$\label{red:order-product} \nu_\delta(fg)=\nu_\delta(f)+\nu_\delta(g).$$ If $\delta(h)\ne0$, then $\nu_\delta(\delta(h))=\nu_\delta(h)-1$. Consequently, $\ker\delta$ is factorially closed in $T$: if $0\ne fg\in\ker\delta$, then $f,g\in\ker\delta$. Moreover, $\delta(h)\ne0$ implies $\delta(h)\notin hT$.
  - Explanation: Product additivity of nilpotence order gives factorial closure and excludes a nonzero derivative divisible by its input.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-extracted-lnd)): If $A\cong\mathbb C^{[4]}$, there are a nonzero locally nilpotent derivation $E$ of $\widetilde R$ and an integer $q\le0$ such that $E$ is homogeneous of fiber weight shift $q$ and $$E^2(v)=0.$$ Here homogeneity means that $E(r)$ has fiber weight $\mathop{\mathrm{wt}}(r)+q$ whenever $r$ is fiber homogeneous and $E(r)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Polynomiality would produce a nonzero locally nilpotent derivation on the determinant ring with nonpositive fiber shift and vanishing second iterate on the principal generator.
- **C14** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-red-sign-essential)): The restriction to nonpositive fiber weight is essential to the obstruction proved in [sec:rigidity](#sec:rigidity). Indeed, the derivation $$E_+(b)=a,\qquad E_+(c)=d,\qquad E_+(a)=E_+(d)=E_+(u)=0$$ annihilates $ac-bd-1$ and so defines a derivation of $\widetilde R$. It is locally nilpotent, since on the polynomial ring it lowers total degree in $b,c$, and it has fiber weight shift $+2$. Nevertheless, $E_+(v)=a^4$ and $E_+^2(v)=0$.
  - Explanation: A positive fiber shift provides an explicit derivation satisfying the second-iterate condition, so the sign hypothesis is essential.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-rig-abc)): Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero, and let $X,Y,Z\in F[q]$ be nonzero, pairwise relatively prime polynomials satisfying $X+Y=Z$. If they are not all constant, then $$\max\{\deg X,\deg Y,\deg Z\} \le r(XYZ)-1,$$ where $r(XYZ)$ is the number of distinct roots of $XYZ$ in an algebraic closure of $F$.
  - Explanation: The polynomial root-count bound controls the orbit polynomials used in the rigidity argument.
- **C16** (classification, [source](#stmt-rig-one-variable)): Let $F$ be a field of characteristic zero. If $\delta$ is a locally nilpotent $F$-derivation of $F[t]$, then $\delta(t)\in F$.
  - Explanation: A locally nilpotent derivation of a polynomial algebra over its coefficient field sends the variable to a constant.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-rigidity)): There is no nonzero locally nilpotent $\mathbb C$-derivation $E$ of $\widetilde R$ with a fixed fiber shift $e\le0$ and $E^2(v)=0$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary highest component, polynomial orbit bound and nonsquare obstruction exclude the constrained derivation.
- **C18** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-cor-affine-fibrations)): Let $P,H,A$ be as in <a href="#eq:example" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:example">[eq:example]</a>, and put $X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A$. Write $\mathbb A^1_p=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathbb C[p]$ and $\mathbb A^2_{p,q}=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\mathbb C[p,q]$, where $q$ is independent of $p$. Consider the morphisms $$\pi:X\longrightarrow\mathbb A^1_p, \qquad \rho=(p,H):\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}P=\mathbb A^5\longrightarrow\mathbb A^2_{p,q},$$ induced respectively by $\mathbb C[p]\to A$ and by $\mathbb C[p,q]\to P$ with $q\mapsto H$. Both are smooth surjective morphisms of relative dimension three. For either morphism and every point $y$ of its base, the fiber over $y$ is isomorphic to $\mathbb A^3_{\kappa(y)}$; in particular, every geometric fiber is affine three-space. Neither morphism is a Zariski-locally trivial $\mathbb A^3$-bundle. Thus the Dolgachev–Weisfeiler affine-fibration conjecture fails in relative dimension three over each of the smooth complex affine bases $\mathbb A^1$ and $\mathbb A^2$.
  - Explanation: Residual coordinates give smooth affine-space fibers, while nonpolynomiality blocks Zariski local triviality.

        "anchor": "stmt-deg-valuation",
        "exact_quote": "The map $S\\to A$ is injective, and $S[p^{-1}]=A[p^{-1}]$. The ring $R$ is a smooth domain. On the common fraction field of $S$ and $A$ there is a discrete valuation $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_p$, normalized by $\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_p(p)=1$, coming from $S_{pS}$. For $b\\in S$ and every integer $i\\geq 0$, $$\\label{deg:global-divisibility}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_p(b)\\geq i \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad b\\in p^iS.$$ In particular, each nonzero $h\\in S[p^{-1}]$ has a unique expression $h=p^m b$ with $m=\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)\\in\\mathbb Z$ and $b\\in S\\setminus pS$.",
        "source_id": "PM-047.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Under [deg:graded-injection](#deg:graded-injection), the associated graded algebra is the following specific subalgebra of $R[\\tau,\\tau^{-1}]$: $$\\label{deg:G}\n G:=\\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}A\n   =R[\\tau,I/\\tau^2]\n   =R[\\tau,f/\\tau^2,g/\\tau^2].$$ It is a smooth domain. Give $P=\\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J]$ the presentation weights $$\\label{deg:presentation-weights}\n (\\omega(p),\\omega(s),\\omega(u),\\omega(F),\\omega(J))\n       =(-1,0,0,2,2).$$ Then every nonzero $h\\in A$ has a polynomial representative whose maximum weight is exactly $\\deg h$, and there is a graded isomorphism $$\\label{deg:graded-presentation}\n G\\cong P/(H_{\\mathrm{top}}),\\qquad\n H_{\\mathrm{top}}=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2,\n \\quad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F.$$ The images of $p,s,u,F,J$ in this isomorphism are respectively $\\tau,s_R,u,f/\\tau^2,g/\\tau^2$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-principalization",
        "exact_quote": "The morphism $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\widetilde R\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ is the complement of the zero section in a line bundle, with fiber scaling specified by the weights $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(a)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(d)=1,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(b)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(c)=-1,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(u)=0.$$ The homomorphism $R\\to\\widetilde R$ is faithfully flat, and $I\\widetilde R=(v)$. Moreover, base change gives a faithfully flat inclusion $G\\hookrightarrow\\widetilde G$, where $$\\label{bun:pullback-algebra}\n \\widetilde G:=G\\otimes_R\\widetilde R\n   =\\widetilde R[\\tau,v/\\tau^2]\\subset\\widetilde R[\\tau,\\tau^{-1}],\\qquad\n \\widetilde G\\cong\\widetilde R[\\tau,V]/(\\tau^2V-v).$$ Thus $V$ denotes the element $v/\\tau^2$ in the displayed subalgebra. The ring $\\widetilde G$ is a domain and has the following commuting gradings:*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n|                    | *$a,d$* | *$b,c$* | *$u$* | *$\\tau$* | *$V$* |\n|:------------------:|--------:|--------:|------:|---------:|------:|\n| *Valuation degree* |   *$0$* |   *$0$* | *$0$* |   *$-1$* | *$2$* |\n|   *Fiber weight*   |   *$1$* |  *$-1$* | *$0$* |    *$0$* | *$2$* |\n\n</div>",
        "source_id": "PM-047.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "The morphism $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\widetilde R\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}R$ is the complement of the zero section in a line bundle, with fiber scaling specified by the weights $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(a)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(d)=1,\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(b)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(c)=-1,\\qquad \\mathop{\\mathrm{wt}}(u)=0.$$ The homomorphism $R\\to\\widetilde R$ is faithfully flat, and $I\\widetilde R=(v)$. Moreover, base change gives a faithfully flat inclusion $G\\hookrightarrow\\widetilde G$, where $$\\label{bun:pullback-algebra}\n \\widetilde G:=G\\otimes_R\\widetilde R\n   =\\widetilde R[\\tau,v/\\tau^2]\\subset\\widetilde R[\\tau,\\tau^{-1}],\\qquad\n \\widetilde G\\cong\\widetilde R[\\tau,V]/(\\tau^2V-v).$$ Thus $V$ denotes the element $v/\\tau^2$ in the displayed subalgebra. The ring $\\widetilde G$ is a domain and has the following commuting gradings:*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n|                    | *$a,d$* | *$b,c$* | *$u$* | *$\\tau$* | *$V$* |\n|:------------------:|--------:|--------:|------:|---------:|------:|\n| *Valuation degree* |   *$0$* |   *$0$* | *$0$* |   *$-1$* | *$2$* |\n|   *Fiber weight*   |   *$1$* |  *$-1$* | *$0$* |    *$0$* | *$2$* |\n\n</div>",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-linearization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth integral affine variety over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\pi\\colon\\mathbb A^1\\times X\\to X$ be the projection. Then $$\\pi^*\\colon\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n                  \\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(\\mathbb A^1\\times X).$$ For every algebraic additive action $\\alpha\\colon\\mathbb G_a\\times X\\to X$ and every line bundle $L$ on $X$, there is a unique isomorphism of line bundles $$\\label{bun:linearization-map}\n \\phi\\colon\\pi^*L\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\alpha^*L$$ whose restriction to $\\{0\\}\\times X$ is $\\mathrm{id}_L$. This isomorphism obeys the action cocycle. It therefore defines a unique action on $L$ that lifts $\\alpha$, is linear on its fibers, and acts as the identity at the group identity. The action preserves $L^\\times$ and commutes with fiber scaling. If $L^\\times$ is affine, the induced derivation of its coordinate ring is locally nilpotent and preserves the fiber weights.",
        "source_id": "PM-047.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-linearization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth integral affine variety over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\pi\\colon\\mathbb A^1\\times X\\to X$ be the projection. Then $$\\pi^*\\colon\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\n                  \\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(\\mathbb A^1\\times X).$$ For every algebraic additive action $\\alpha\\colon\\mathbb G_a\\times X\\to X$ and every line bundle $L$ on $X$, there is a unique isomorphism of line bundles $$\\label{bun:linearization-map}\n \\phi\\colon\\pi^*L\\xrightarrow{\\ \\sim\\ }\\alpha^*L$$ whose restriction to $\\{0\\}\\times X$ is $\\mathrm{id}_L$. This isomorphism obeys the action cocycle. It therefore defines a unique action on $L$ that lifts $\\alpha$, is linear on its fibers, and acts as the identity at the group identity. The action preserves $L^\\times$ and commutes with fiber scaling. If $L^\\times$ is affine, the induced derivation of its coordinate ring is locally nilpotent and preserves the fiber weights.",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a smooth integral affine variety over $\\mathbb C$, and let $\\pi\\colon\\mathbb A^1\\times X\\to X$ be the projection. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Polynomial extension notation** ([source](#eq:cancellation-question)): Here $C^{[r]}$ denotes a polynomial ring in $r$ variables over a ring $C$.
- **Defining polynomial and quotient algebra** ([source](#eq:example)): Let $p,s,u,F,J$ be algebraically independent over $\mathbb C$, and set $$\begin{split} P&=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J],\qquad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F,\\ H&=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2-pu,\qquad A=P/(H). \end{split} \label{eq:example}$$
- **Residual and one-stable coordinates** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A polynomial in $\mathbb C[p]^{[4]}$ is a *residual coordinate* if its image is a polynomial coordinate over the residue field of every prime of $\mathbb C[p]$; it is *one-stably a coordinate* if it becomes a coordinate after adjoining one variable.
- **Auxiliary and coefficient algebras** ([source](#deg:S-R)): Consider the presented algebra $$\label{deg:S-R} S=\mathbb C[p,x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)-p^3u\bigr), \qquad R=S/pS=\mathbb C[x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)\bigr).$$
- **Valuation degree** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Its negative defines the *valuation degree* $\deg h=-\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)$ and a filtration of $A$.
- **Valuation filtration and associated graded algebra** ([source](#pm-h-13)): These formulas give an exhaustive, separated filtration indexed by all integers, $$\mathcal F_dA=\{h\in A:\deg h\leq d\},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb Z}\mathcal F_dA/\mathcal F_{d-1}A.$$
- **Center ideal** ([source](#pm-h-14)): In $R$ set $$\label{deg:center} s_R=y-x(x-u^3),\qquad f=x-s_R^2-u^3,\qquad g=z-s_Rx,\qquad I=(f,g).$$
- **Complex derivation** ([source](#pm-h-15)): A $\mathbb C$-derivation of a $\mathbb C$-algebra $C$ is a $\mathbb C$-linear map $D:C\to C$ satisfying $D(hh')=hD(h')+h'D(h)$.
- **Local nilpotence** ([source](#pm-h-15)): It is *locally nilpotent* if for each $h\in C$ there is an integer $N\geq 1$ such that $D^Nh=0$; all subsequent iterates then vanish as well.
- **Homogeneous degree shift** ([source](#pm-h-15)): On a graded algebra a derivation is *homogeneous of degree shift $j$* if it sends the component of degree $d$ to that of degree $d+j$ for every $d\in\mathbb Z$.
- **Determinant algebra and principal generator** ([source](#bun:determinant-ring)): Set $$\label{bun:determinant-ring} \widetilde R=\mathbb C[a,d,b,c,u]/(ac-bd-1),\qquad x=ab,\quad y=dc,\quad z=db,$$ and let $$\label{bun:v} v=a^2(x-u^3)-d^2=a^3b-a^2u^3-d^2.$$
- **Picard group** ([source](#pm-h-18)): For a variety $X$, $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)$ is the group of isomorphism classes of line bundles on $X$, with tensor product as its operation.
- **Normalized bundle lift** ([source](#pm-h-18)): For a line bundle $L$ on $X$, normalization means that the restriction over $\{0\}\times X$ is the identity on $L$.
- **Locally nilpotent derivation order** ([source](#stmt-lem-lnd-order)): For $0\ne h\in T$, define $$\nu_\delta(h)=\max\{n\ge0:\delta^n(h)\ne0\}.$$

          "exact_quote": "These formulas give an exhaustive, separated filtration indexed by all integers, $$\\mathcal F_dA=\\{h\\in A:\\deg h\\leq d\\},\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}A=\\bigoplus_{d\\in\\mathbb Z}\\mathcal F_dA/\\mathcal F_{d-1}A.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "These formulas give an exhaustive, separated filtration indexed by all integers, $$\\mathcal F_dA=\\{h\\in A:\\deg h\\leq d\\},\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{gr}}A=\\bigoplus_{d\\in\\mathbb Z}\\mathcal F_dA/\\mathcal F_{d-1}A.$$",
      "interpretation": "The integer-indexed filtration determines the homogeneous degeneration.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Valuation filtration and associated graded algebra",
      "scope": "The valuation filtration of the quotient algebra."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-14",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-14",
          "exact_quote": "In $R$ set $$\\label{deg:center}\n s_R=y-x(x-u^3),\\qquad\n f=x-s_R^2-u^3,\\qquad\n g=z-s_Rx,\\qquad I=(f,g).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "In $R$ set $$\\label{deg:center}\n s_R=y-x(x-u^3),\\qquad\n f=x-s_R^2-u^3,\\qquad\n g=z-s_Rx,\\qquad I=(f,g).$$",
      "interpretation": "The center ideal is generated by the residual numerators of the modified coordinates.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Center ideal",
      "scope": "The specified coefficient algebra."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-15",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-15",
          "exact_quote": "A $\\mathbb C$-derivation of a $\\mathbb C$-algebra $C$ is a $\\mathbb C$-linear map $D:C\\to C$ satisfying $D(hh')=hD(h')+h'D(h)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A $\\mathbb C$-derivation of a $\\mathbb C$-algebra $C$ is a $\\mathbb C$-linear map $D:C\\to C$ satisfying $D(hh')=hD(h')+h'D(h)$.",
      "interpretation": "Derivations are scalar-linear maps satisfying the product rule.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Complex derivation",
      "scope": "Complex algebras."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-15",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-15",
          "exact_quote": "It is *locally nilpotent* if for each $h\\in C$ there is an integer $N\\geq 1$ such that $D^Nh=0$; all subsequent iterates then vanish as well.",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "It is *locally nilpotent* if for each $h\\in C$ there is an integer $N\\geq 1$ such that $D^Nh=0$; all subsequent iterates then vanish as well.",
      "interpretation": "Each element has a finite nilpotence order; no uniform bound is asserted.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Local nilpotence",
      "scope": "The preceding complex derivation."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-15",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-15",
          "exact_quote": "On a graded algebra a derivation is *homogeneous of degree shift $j$* if it sends the component of degree $d$ to that of degree $d+j$ for every $d\\in\\mathbb Z$.",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "On a graded algebra a derivation is *homogeneous of degree shift $j$* if it sends the component of degree $d$ to that of degree $d+j$ for every $d\\in\\mathbb Z$.",
      "interpretation": "A fixed degree shift determines the image component of each homogeneous input.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Homogeneous degree shift",
      "scope": "Integer-graded algebras."

          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }

      "interpretation": "The determinant algebra realizes the torus bundle and the displayed generator principalizes the center.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R3** [claim](#stmt-con-domain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-con-domain): Localization and unique factorization -> Integrality and the Laurent polynomial presentation.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-stabilization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stabilization): Finite exponential shift, determinant-one change, divisibility certificate and polynomial inverse -> Explicit polynomial coordinates on the cylinder.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-deg-valuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-deg-valuation): Localization, primality and parameter divisibility -> The normalized parameter valuation and its coefficient algebra.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-graded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-graded): Kernel saturation, homogeneous weight reduction and the Jacobian criterion -> The exact smooth graded presentation over the specified coefficient ring.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-induced-lnd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-induced-lnd): A maximal generator shift and exact-weight representatives -> A nonzero homogeneous locally nilpotent derivation preserving invariant initial forms.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-invariant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-invariant): Contradiction through polynomial coordinates and coordinate differentiation -> A positive valuation degree invariant if the original algebra is polynomial.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-principalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-principalization): Laurent bundle charts and global principalization identities -> A faithfully flat punctured line-bundle pullback with separate gradings.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-prop-linearization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-linearization): Divisor classes, polynomial units and normalization -> A unique normalized fiber-linear additive lift.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-prop-homogeneous-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-homogeneous-lift): Normalized lift uniqueness under conjugation by degree scaling -> A nonzero lift with the same valuation shift and fiber weight zero.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-lem-lnd-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lnd-order): The iterated product rule in a characteristic-zero domain -> Factorial closure and additive nilpotence order.
- **R13** [claim](#stmt-prop-extracted-lnd) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-extracted-lnd): Homogeneous component formulas, invariant factors and a decreasing nilpotence order -> A nonzero determinant-ring derivation with nonpositive fiber shift and vanishing second iterate.
- **R14** [claim](#stmt-red-sign-essential) / [proof](#stmt-red-sign-essential): Explicit positive-weight locally nilpotent derivation -> The sign restriction cannot be removed.
- **R15** [claim](#stmt-rig-abc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rig-abc): Wronskian multiplicities and root counting -> The polynomial degree bound.
- **R16** [claim](#stmt-rig-one-variable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-rig-one-variable): Nonvanishing leading coefficients of iterated derivations -> A locally nilpotent coefficient-field derivation sends the variable to a constant.
- **R17** [claim](#stmt-prop-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rigidity): Highest auxiliary component, polynomial orbit bound, nonsquare order and coefficient intersection -> The extracted constrained derivation cannot exist.
- **R1** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rigidity-2): Contradiction between extracted action and weighted rigidity -> The complex affine-space cancellation counterexample.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-cor-stable-coordinate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-stable-coordinate): Residual-coordinate stabilization and quotienting an ambient coordinate -> Failure of the stable-coordinate implication for the specified polynomial.
- **R18** [claim](#stmt-cor-affine-fibrations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-affine-fibrations): Residual-coordinate fibers, the smoothness criterion and symmetric algebra freeness -> Smooth affine-space fibrations without Zariski local triviality.

          "exact_quote": "Let $P,H,A$ be as in <a href=\"#eq:example\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:example\">[eq:example]</a>, and put $X=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}A$. Write $\\mathbb A^1_p=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\mathbb C[p]$ and $\\mathbb A^2_{p,q}=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}\\mathbb C[p,q]$, where $q$ is independent of $p$. Consider the morphisms $$\\pi:X\\longrightarrow\\mathbb A^1_p,\n \\qquad\n \\rho=(p,H):\\mathop{\\mathrm{Spec}}P=\\mathbb A^5\\longrightarrow\\mathbb A^2_{p,q},$$ induced respectively by $\\mathbb C[p]\\to A$ and by $\\mathbb C[p,q]\\to P$ with $q\\mapsto H$. Both are smooth surjective morphisms of relative dimension three. For either morphism and every point $y$ of its base, the fiber over $y$ is isomorphic to $\\mathbb A^3_{\\kappa(y)}$; in particular, every geometric fiber is affine three-space. Neither morphism is a Zariski-locally trivial $\\mathbb A^3$-bundle. Thus the Dolgachev–Weisfeiler affine-fibration conjecture fails in relative dimension three over each of the smooth complex affine bases $\\mathbb A^1$ and $\\mathbb A^2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-047.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R18",
      "inputs": [
        "The specified integral complex fourfold has a polynomial cylinder but is not affine space.",
        "The specified polynomial becomes a coordinate after stabilization although it is not an ambient coordinate.",
        "An exponential shift and polynomial inverse give explicit coordinates on the cylinder."
      ],
      "method": "Residual-coordinate fibers, the smoothness criterion and symmetric algebra freeness",
      "output": "Smooth affine-space fibrations without Zariski local triviality.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-affine-fibrations"
    }
  ]
}

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **linear_coordinates**: The displayed linear change in F,J has a polynomial inverse.
- **stabilization**: Adjoining the independent variable w gives a polynomial cylinder.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **positive_shift**: Apply the rigidity obstruction without restricting fiber weight. -> The displayed positive-weight derivation satisfies the same second-iterate equation.
- **partial_certificate**: Treat the linear coordinate calculation as the complete cancellation proof. -> The cylinder proof also requires generation and algebraic independence, and nonpolynomiality requires the rigidity argument.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The displayed linear coordinate change and its polynomial inverse admit a finite exact certificate. This covers a step of the stabilization argument.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of integer quadruples [s,u,F,J], at most 256 rows.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each row contains four integers; no floats or booleans.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rows [L,M,recovered_F,recovered_J].
# TIME: Linear in row count with exact integer multiplication cost.
# SPACE: Linear in row count and integer bit lengths.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact python-flint fmpz integers.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits nonzero.
import json,sys
from flint import fmpz
rows=json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
assert isinstance(rows,list) and len(rows)<=256
answer=[]
for row in rows:
    assert isinstance(row,list) and len(row)==4 and all(type(v) is int for v in row)
    s,u,F,J=map(fmpz,row)
    x0=s*s+u**3
    L=x0*x0*F-(1+2*s*x0)*J
    M=(1-2*s*x0)*F+4*s*s*J
    recovered_F=4*s*s*L+(1+2*s*x0)*M
    recovered_J=-(1-2*s*x0)*L+x0*x0*M
    answer.append([int(L),int(M),int(recovered_F),int(recovered_J)])
print(json.dumps(answer,separators=(',',':')))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-complex** (hypothesis): The construction and the additive actions are defined over the complex numbers. Blocked use: ['Extending the stated counterexample to arbitrary ground fields without a separate argument.']
- **L-dimension** (counterexample_scope): The cancellation counterexample concerns dimension four. Blocked use: ['Claiming failure of complex affine-space cancellation in every dimension from this example.']
- **L-stable** (counterexample_scope): The Stable Coordinate counterexample concerns the specified polynomial in ambient dimension five after one stabilization. Blocked use: ['Treating every polynomial that becomes a coordinate after stabilization as a counterexample.']
- **L-localization** (edge_case): The localized coordinate substitutions require the localization parameter to be invertible. The polynomial representative is needed in the unlocalized ring. Blocked use: ['Dividing by the localization parameter on its vanishing fiber.']
- **L-sign** (hypothesis): Nonpositive fiber shift is essential to the rigidity obstruction; positive fiber shift admits the displayed locally nilpotent derivation. Blocked use: ['Applying the rigidity obstruction after dropping its fiber-shift condition.']
- **L-coefficients** (edge_case): The induced homogeneous additive action need not preserve the coefficient subalgebra. Blocked use: ['Constructing the additive lift by assuming that the action descends to the quadric base.']
- **L-gradings** (hypothesis): Valuation degree and fiber weight remain distinct throughout the argument. Blocked use: ['Using valuation degree as fiber weight in the extraction or rigidity obstruction.']
- **L-abc** (hypothesis): The polynomial degree inequality requires characteristic zero, nonzero pairwise relatively prime polynomials and a nonconstant triple. Blocked use: ['Applying the polynomial inequality to a constant triple or without checking relative primality.']
- **L-original-field** (hypothesis): The nonsquare obstruction uses the original fraction field; algebraic closure is used only for counting roots. Blocked use: ['Discarding the original-field restriction on the leading coefficients.']
- **L-fibrations** (counterexample_scope): The affine-fibration counterexamples have relative dimension three over the stated complex affine bases. Blocked use: ['Claiming that these counterexamples settle the corresponding assertions in relative dimensions one or two.']
- **L-linear-lift** (hypothesis): The normalized additive lift concerns line bundles over a smooth integral affine complex base and is linear on fibers. Blocked use: ['Extending the stated lifting theorem to arbitrary singular bases or arbitrary higher-rank bundles.']

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

namespace ComplexCancellation

abbrev P := MvPolynomial (Fin 5) ℂ

def p : P := MvPolynomial.X 0
def s : P := MvPolynomial.X 1
def u : P := MvPolynomial.X 2
def F : P := MvPolynomial.X 3
def J : P := MvPolynomial.X 4
def x : P := s ^ 2 + u ^ 3 + p ^ 2 * F
def H : P := x ^ 2 * F - (1 + 2 * s * x) * J - p ^ 2 * J ^ 2 - p * u
abbrev A := P ⧸ Ideal.span {H}

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  Algebra.FiniteType ℂ A ∧ IsDomain A ∧ ringKrullDim A = 4 ∧
  Nonempty (Polynomial A ≃ₐ[ℂ] MvPolynomial (Fin 5) ℂ) ∧
  ¬ Nonempty (A ≃ₐ[ℂ] MvPolynomial (Fin 4) ℂ)

theorem main : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end ComplexCancellation

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct an explicit integral complex affine fourfold <i>X</i> with $`X\times\mathbb A^1\cong\mathbb A^5`$ but $`X\not\cong\mathbb A^4`$. This gives a negative answer to Zariski's affine-space cancellation problem over ℂ in dimension four. The same construction disproves the Stable Coordinate Conjecture in ambient dimension five and yields smooth 𝔸<sup>3</sup>-fibrations over 𝔸<sup>1</sup> and 𝔸<sup>2</sup> that are not Zariski-locally trivial. These fibrations disprove the Dolgachev–Weisfeiler affine-fibration conjecture over these bases.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

<a id="eq:cancellation-question"></a>
The Zariski cancellation problem asks whether an affine variety is determined by its cylinder when that cylinder is affine space. Over the complex numbers, its algebraic formulation is the implication $$A[w]\simeq \mathbb C^{[n+1]}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 A\simeq\mathbb C^{[n]},
 \label{eq:cancellation-question}$$ where $A$ is a finitely generated commutative unital complex algebra, $w$ is an independent polynomial variable, and all isomorphisms fix the complex scalars. Here $C^{[r]}$ denotes a polynomial ring in $r$ variables over a ring $C$. The hypothesis says that adjoining one variable turns $A$ into a polynomial ring; the question is whether that variable can be cancelled. An isomorphism of cylinders need not respect the given projection to the affine line, so its existence alone does not identify polynomial coordinates on $A$.

Cancellation for the affine line follows from the work of Abhyankar, Heinzer, and Eakin <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[AEH](#ref-AEH), Corollary (2.8)]. Cancellation for the complex affine plane was established through the work of Fujita, Miyanishi, and Sugie <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Fujita](#ref-Fujita), [MiyanishiSugie](#ref-MiyanishiSugie)], using the structure of affine surfaces and cylinderlike open sets. In positive characteristic, Asanuma’s work on polynomial fiber rings supplied threefolds whose one-variable cylinders are affine four-space <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Asanuma](#ref-Asanuma)]. Gupta proved that suitable members of this family are not affine three-space over their ground field <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[GuptaThreefold](#ref-GuptaThreefold)], then established failure of cancellation in every dimension at least three over every field of positive characteristic <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GuptaPositive](#ref-GuptaPositive)]. The latter result uses a separate family of examples: adjoining variables to a threefold whose first cylinder is already polynomial would not produce higher-dimensional counterexamples.

The characteristic-zero problem in dimensions at least three is still listed as open in Gaifullin–Petrov’s July 2026 preprint <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GaifullinPetrov](#ref-GaifullinPetrov), p. 1]. Gupta’s survey <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GuptaSurvey](#ref-GuptaSurvey)] explains its history and its connections with coordinates, polynomial fibrations, and the recognition of affine space. We give a negative answer to <a href="#eq:cancellation-question" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cancellation-question">[eq:cancellation-question]</a> over $\mathbb C$ for $n=4$. The defining polynomial also gives a negative answer to the Stable Coordinate Conjecture over $\mathbb C$ in ambient dimension five, already after adjoining one variable.

<a id="eq:example"></a>
Let $p,s,u,F,J$ be algebraically independent over $\mathbb C$, and set $$\begin{split}
 P&=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J],\qquad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F,\\
 H&=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2-pu,\qquad A=P/(H).
 \end{split}
 \label{eq:example}$$ Thus $x$ is an abbreviation for the displayed polynomial, and $H$ is a polynomial in exactly the five indicated variables.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *The algebra $A$ in <a href="#eq:example" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:example">[eq:example]</a> is an integral complex algebra of dimension four. For an independent variable $w$, $$A[w]\simeq\mathbb C^{[5]},
 \qquad
 A\not\simeq\mathbb C^{[4]}.$$ Consequently the Zariski cancellation problem over the complex numbers has a negative answer in dimension four.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### The polynomial cylinder.

The stabilization belongs to the theory of residual and stable coordinates. A polynomial in $\mathbb C[p]^{[4]}$ is a *residual coordinate* if its image is a polynomial coordinate over the residue field of every prime of $\mathbb C[p]$; it is *one-stably a coordinate* if it becomes a coordinate after adjoining one variable. Our $H$ is a residual coordinate: it is a coordinate after inverting $p$, while a linear change in $F,J$ makes its reduction at $p=0$ a coordinate. Its one-stability therefore follows from the theorem of El Kahoui–Ouali <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ElKahouiOuali](#ref-ElKahouiOuali), Theorem 1.1], generalized by Dutta–Lahiri <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DuttaLahiri](#ref-DuttaLahiri), Theorem 3.4].

The Stable Coordinate Conjecture asks whether a polynomial $f\in\mathbb C^{[n]}$ that becomes a coordinate in $\mathbb C^{[n+1]}$ after adjoining one variable must already be a coordinate in $\mathbb C^{[n]}$ <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[MakarLimanovVanRossumShpilrainYu](#ref-MakarLimanovVanRossumShpilrainYu), p. 342]. The assertion concerns polynomial automorphisms of the ambient spaces.

<a id="stmt-cor-stable-coordinate"></a>
<div id="cor:stable-coordinate" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Failure of the Stable Coordinate Conjecture). *The polynomial $H$ in <a href="#eq:example" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:example">[eq:example]</a> is not a coordinate of $P=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J]$, but it is a coordinate of $P[w]$ for one independent variable $w$. Thus the Stable Coordinate Conjecture over $\mathbb C$ fails in ambient dimension five after one stabilization.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-stable-coordinate"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-stable-coordinate</summary>

*Proof.* Since $H$ is a residual coordinate of $P=\mathbb C[p]^{[4]}$, the theorem of Dutta–Lahiri <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DuttaLahiri](#ref-DuttaLahiri), Theorem 3.4], applied with $R=\mathbb C[p]$ and four relative variables, gives $G_2,G_3,G_4,G_5\in P[w]$ such that $$P[w]=\mathbb C[p][H,G_2,G_3,G_4,G_5]
     =\mathbb C[p,H,G_2,G_3,G_4,G_5].$$ Hence $H$ is a coordinate of $P[w]$ as a $\mathbb C$-algebra. If $H$ were a coordinate of $P$ as a $\mathbb C$-algebra, quotienting a polynomial coordinate system containing $H$ by $(H)$ would give $A=P/(H)\simeq\mathbb C^{[4]}$, contrary to [thm:main](#thm:main). ◻

</details>

A derivation $D$ is *locally nilpotent* if every element is killed by some iterate of $D$; over $\mathbb C$, its exponential is an algebraic action of the additive group $\mathbb G_a$. We make the stabilization method explicit. A locally nilpotent derivation of $P$ sends $H$ to $p^3$. Exponentiating it converts $H$ into $H+p^3w$, and a coordinate calculation identifies the resulting quotient with a polynomial ring. This exponential-shift argument follows the method of El Kahoui–Ouali <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ElKahouiOuali](#ref-ElKahouiOuali), Lemma 3.1 and proof of Theorem 1.1]; the final coordinate change realizes the transfer criterion of Edo–Vénéreau <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[EdoVenereau](#ref-EdoVenereau), Theorem 7]. We include its polynomial inverse and the divisibility that makes it valid at $p=0$.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Affine-space fibrations.

An $\mathbb A^3$-fibration is a morphism of complex affine varieties whose base change to every geometric point of the target is affine three-space. The affine-fibration assertion of Dolgachev and Weisfeiler predicts that an affine morphism to a normal locally noetherian integral base is Zariski-locally a product with affine $r$-space if every fiber is affine $r$-space over its residue field <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DolgachevWeisfeiler](#ref-DolgachevWeisfeiler), Section 3.8.5]. The residual-coordinate property of $H$ gives two counterexamples in relative dimension three: $$p:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}A\longrightarrow\mathbb A^1,
 \qquad
 (p,H):\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}P=\mathbb A^5\longrightarrow\mathbb A^2.$$ Both maps are smooth $\mathbb A^3$-fibrations, but neither is a Zariski-locally trivial $\mathbb A^3$-bundle. [sec:affine-fibrations](#sec:affine-fibrations) proves this consequence using the locally-polynomial-algebra theorem of Bass–Connell–Wright <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BassConnellWright](#ref-BassConnellWright), Theorem 3(a)] and the Quillen–Suslin theorem <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Suslin](#ref-Suslin), Theorem 2(b)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### From polynomiality to a homogeneous additive action.

The main work is to prove that $A$ itself is not polynomial. Additive actions have long served to distinguish affine varieties from affine space: Makar-Limanov used them to show that the Russell cubic is not affine three-space <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[MakarLimanov](#ref-MakarLimanov)]. Our obstruction has three stages, beginning with the passage from filtered derivations to homogeneous derivations developed by Kaliman and Makar-Limanov <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[KalimanMakarLimanov](#ref-KalimanMakarLimanov), Sections 3–5].

The construction in [sec:construction](#sec:construction), [sec:degeneration](#sec:degeneration) identifies an auxiliary subalgebra of $A$ with the presentation $$S=\mathbb C[p,x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)-p^3u\bigr),
 \qquad S[p^{-1}]=A[p^{-1}].$$ Under this inclusion, $p^2F$ and $p^2J$ belong to $S$. Define $$R=S/pS=\mathbb C[x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)\bigr),\qquad
 I=(\overline{p^2F},\overline{p^2J})\subset R,$$ where the bars denote residues of elements of $S$ modulo $pS$. The order $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p$ along $p=0$ in $S$ applies to every nonzero element of $A$. Its negative defines the *valuation degree* $\deg h=-\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)$ and a filtration of $A$. We prove the exact identification $$G=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A=R[\tau,I/\tau^2]\subset R[\tau,\tau^{-1}],
 \qquad \deg\tau=-1,\qquad \deg R=0.$$ Here $\tau$ is an indeterminate representing the initial form of $p$, and the right-hand side is generated by $R$, $\tau$, and all $i/\tau^2$ with $i\in I$. The coefficient inclusion is the one induced by $S\subset A$: the residue of $b\in S$ corresponds to its degree-zero class. This is an affine modification of $R[\tau]$ in the sense of Kaliman–Zaidenberg <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[KalimanZaidenberg](#ref-KalimanZaidenberg)], and it is smooth.

If $A$ were polynomial, some polynomial coordinate would have positive valuation degree: otherwise all of $A$ would have nonpositive degree, contrary to $\deg F=2$. Differentiation in a different coordinate fixes that positive-degree coordinate. The exact graded identification then induces a nonzero homogeneous locally nilpotent derivation of $G$ with a nonzero homogeneous invariant of positive degree. This is the action the remaining two stages exclude.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Lifting through the principalizing line bundle.

The standard torus bundle from $\mathrm{SL}_2$ to the smooth affine quadric principalizes $I$ after adjoining the unchanged variable $u$. This quadric and its polynomial embeddings also occur in the work of Blanc and van Santen <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[BlancVanSanten](#ref-BlancVanSanten)]. The bundle algebra and the pullback of $G$ are $$\begin{gathered}
 \widetilde R=\mathbb C[a,d,b,c,u]/(ac-bd-1),\qquad
 x=ab,\quad y=dc,\quad z=db,\\
 \widetilde G=G\otimes_R\widetilde R
     =\widetilde R[\tau,V]/(\tau^2V-v),\qquad
 v=a^3b-a^2u^3-d^2.
 \end{gathered}$$ The valuation degree is zero on $\widetilde R$, with $\deg\tau=-1$ and $\deg V=2$. Separately, fiber scaling gives $a,d$ weight $1$, $b,c$ weight $-1$, and $u,\tau$ weight $0$; thus $v,V$ have fiber weight $2$.

The morphism $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\widetilde G\to\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}G$ is the complement of the zero section in a line bundle. Every additive action on the smooth affine base $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}G$ lifts uniquely to an action on this line bundle that is linear on fibers and normalized at the identity. For the homogeneous action just obtained, uniqueness makes the lift homogeneous with the same valuation degree shift, while fiber linearity preserves fiber weights. This applies even when the original action does not preserve the quadric algebra $R$. A positive-degree invariant is divisible by $V$, forcing $V$ itself to be invariant. The homogeneous pieces of $\widetilde G$, with separate arguments according to whether $\tau$ is invariant, then produce a nonzero locally nilpotent derivation $E$ of $\widetilde R$ with nonpositive fiber weight and $$E^2(v)=0.$$ Thus the lift converts the geometric action into a constrained derivation on the explicit determinant ring.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### The weighted rigidity obstruction.

A highest component $E'$ for an auxiliary grading remains a nonzero locally nilpotent derivation with the same fiber weight. The grading separates the two parts of $v$ so that $(E')^2(d^2+a^2u^3)=0$. The polynomial orbit of $d^2+a^2u^3$ therefore has degree at most one. The Mason–Stothers polynomial $abc$ inequality <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Mason](#ref-Mason), [Stothers](#ref-Stothers)] and the nonsquareness of $-u^3$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}\widetilde R$ force $E'$ to fix $a,d,u$. The determinant relation then forces positive fiber weight, contradicting the sign obtained in the preceding stage. The use of polynomial $abc$ for rigidity of additive actions has precedents in Finston–Maubach <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[FinstonMaubach](#ref-FinstonMaubach), Section 2]; here the nonsquare obstruction requires keeping the leading coefficients in the original fraction field.

<div id="tab:ring-dictionary">

| Ring           | Definition                                                                                                                   | Role in the proof                                    |
|:---------------|:-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|:-----------------------------------------------------|
| $A$            | $P/(H)$                                                                                                                      | Coordinate ring of the fourfold.                     |
| $S$            | <a href="#deg:S-R" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="deg:S-R">[deg:S-R]</a>                                        | Subalgebra of $A$ defining the order along $p=0$.    |
| $R$            | $S/pS$                                                                                                                       | Coefficient ring of the degeneration.                |
| $G$            | $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A=R[\tau,I/\tau^2]$                                                                                     | Exact graded algebra carrying the induced action.    |
| $\widetilde R$ | <a href="#bun:determinant-ring" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bun:determinant-ring">[bun:determinant-ring]</a> | Torus-bundle algebra in which $I$ becomes principal. |
| $\widetilde G$ | $G\otimes_R\widetilde R$                                                                                                     | Pullback algebra carrying the lifted action.         |

The rings in the proof. The valuation degree on $G$ and $\widetilde G$ is separate from the fiber weight on $\widetilde R$ and $\widetilde G$.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Organization.

[tab:ring-dictionary](#tab:ring-dictionary) records the rings used in the argument. [sec:construction](#sec:construction) proves the stabilization. [sec:degeneration](#sec:degeneration) constructs the filtration and identifies $G$. [sec:bundle](#sec:bundle) describes the torus bundle and proves the lifting statement. [sec:reduction](#sec:reduction) extracts the constrained derivation on $\widetilde R$, and [sec:rigidity](#sec:rigidity) excludes it and completes the proof of [thm:main](#thm:main). [sec:affine-fibrations](#sec:affine-fibrations) derives the two nontrivial affine-space fibrations. All locally nilpotent derivations and algebraic group actions in the proof are defined over $\mathbb C$.

<a id="sec:construction"></a>
### The equation and its polynomial cylinder

We first prove that the displayed equation defines an integral fourfold, then construct the five polynomial coordinates on its cylinder. The localization at $p$ explains the equation; the stabilization argument will produce coordinates in the original ring, including the fiber $p=0$.

The construction realizes the residual-coordinate method of El Kahoui–Ouali <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[ElKahouiOuali](#ref-ElKahouiOuali), Lemma 3.1 and Theorem 1.1], generalized by Dutta–Lahiri <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[DuttaLahiri](#ref-DuttaLahiri), Lemma 3.3 and Theorem 3.4]. The coordinate criterion of Maubach <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Maubach](#ref-Maubach), Theorem 4.5] applies to $H+p^3w$: the only root of $p^3$ is $p=0$, where the coordinate change below makes $H$ a coordinate. We retain the explicit formulas so that the resulting isomorphism is available without an existence argument.

Recall the polynomial ring and its elements <a id="con:setup"></a>$$\label{con:setup}
\begin{gathered}
 P=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J],\qquad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F,\\
 y=s+x(x-u^3),\qquad z=sx+p^2J,\\
 H=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2-pu,\qquad A=P/(H).
\end{gathered}$$ Thus $H$ is a specified polynomial in the five independent variables $p,s,u,F,J$. We will use $w$ for the polynomial indeterminate adjoined to $A$. All maps below are $\mathbb C$-algebra maps.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Localization and integrality

The definitions give the identity <a id="con:identity"></a>$$\label{con:identity}
 xy-z(z+1)=p^2(H+pu).$$ Indeed, substituting $y$ and $z$ into the left-hand side and cancelling the terms $sx$ gives $$x^2(x-u^3-s^2)-p^2(1+2sx)J-p^4J^2,$$ which is the right-hand side because $x-u^3-s^2=p^2F$.

<a id="stmt-con-domain"></a>
<div id="con:domain" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *The algebra $A$ is an integral domain of dimension four, $p\ne0$ in $A$, and $$A[p^{-1}]=\mathbb C[p,p^{-1},x,y,z]$$ is a Laurent polynomial ring in the displayed generators.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-con-domain"><summary>Proof of stmt-con-domain</summary>

*Proof.* Over $\mathbb C[p,p^{-1}]$, the elements $x,y,z,u$ are polynomial coordinates on $P[p^{-1}]$. The inverse substitutions are <a id="con:localized-coordinates"></a>$$\label{con:localized-coordinates}
 s=y-x(x-u^3),\qquad
 F=p^{-2}(x-s^2-u^3),\qquad J=p^{-2}(z-sx).$$ Substitution in either direction gives the identity, so in particular the new coordinates are algebraically independent. In these coordinates, [con:identity](#con:identity) reads <a id="con:localized-h"></a>$$\label{con:localized-h}
 H=p^{-2}\bigl(xy-z(z+1)\bigr)-pu.$$ The coefficient of $u$ is a unit. Replacing $u$ by $H$ is therefore another polynomial coordinate change over $\mathbb C[p,p^{-1}]$. Consequently $(H)$ is prime in $P[p^{-1}]$, and its quotient is the Laurent polynomial ring asserted in the lemma, with $u=p^{-3}(xy-z(z+1))$.

Set $x_0=s^2+u^3$. Reduction modulo $p$ gives $$H\bmod p=x_0^2F-(1+2sx_0)J\ne0,$$ so $p$ does not divide $H$ in $P$. To descend irreducibility from the localization, factor $H$ in the unique factorization domain $P$. Its image in $P[p^{-1}]$ is irreducible, so all but one of its irreducible factors must become units there. An irreducible element of $P$ that becomes a unit upon inverting $p$ divides a power of $p$, and hence is associated to $p$. Nondivisibility by $p$ excludes such factors. Thus $H$ is irreducible in $P$, and is prime because $P$ is a unique factorization domain. This proves that $A$ is a domain. Its localization computed above is nonzero, which also proves that $p\ne0$ in $A$. This localization has fraction field $\mathbb C(p,x,y,z)$, of transcendence degree four over $\mathbb C$. The same is true of $A$, so the dimension formula for finitely generated domains over a field <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tag 00P0] gives $\dim A=4$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### An explicit stabilization

The main calculation has two stages. An exponential automorphism replaces $H$ by $H+p^3w$. A linear coordinate change in $F,J$ then makes this new equation linear modulo $p^2$, which allows a polynomial root modulo $p^3$ and a fifth coordinate to be written down explicitly.

<a id="stmt-prop-stabilization"></a>
<div id="prop:stabilization" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4**. *For the algebra $A$ in [con:setup](#con:setup), there is an algebraic $\mathbb C$-isomorphism $$A[w]\cong\mathbb C^{[5]}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-stabilization"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-stabilization</summary>

*Proof.* We first change the equation by a polynomial automorphism, and then exhibit five polynomial coordinates on the resulting quotient.

*Changing the equation.* On $P[p^{-1}]=\mathbb C[p,p^{-1},x,y,z,u]$, define $\Delta=-p^2\partial/\partial u$, with $p,x,y,z$ fixed. This derivation is locally nilpotent: every polynomial is killed by a sufficiently high iterate of $\Delta$. Applying it to [con:localized-coordinates](#con:localized-coordinates) gives <a id="con:derivation"></a>$$\label{con:derivation}
\begin{gathered}
 \Delta p=0,\qquad \Delta u=-p^2,\qquad
 \Delta s=-3p^2xu^2,\\
 \Delta F=(6sx+3)u^2,\qquad \Delta J=3x^2u^2.
\end{gathered}$$ These values belong to $P$, so $\Delta$ restricts to a locally nilpotent derivation of $P$. Extend it by $\Delta w=0$. The formula $$\Phi(f)=\exp(w\Delta)(f)
       =\sum_{i\ge0}\frac{w^i}{i!}\Delta^i(f)
       \qquad(f\in P[w])$$ is a finite sum for every $f$. The iterated Leibniz rule makes $\Phi$ an algebra homomorphism, and the exponential identity gives its inverse $\exp(-w\Delta)$. By [con:localized-h](#con:localized-h), $\Delta H=p^3$ and $\Delta^2H=0$. Hence <a id="con:cylinder-automorphism"></a>$$\label{con:cylinder-automorphism}
 \Phi(H)=H+p^3w,\qquad
 A[w]\xrightarrow[\ \Phi\ ]{\ \sim\ }
 T:=P[w]/(H+p^3w).$$ In particular, $T$ is a domain by [con:domain](#con:domain), and its element $p$ is nonzero since $\Phi$ fixes $p$.

*Coordinates adapted to the fiber $p=0$.* With $x_0=s^2+u^3$, put <a id="con:linear-coordinates"></a>$$\label{con:linear-coordinates}
 L=x_0^2F-(1+2sx_0)J,\qquad
 M=(1-2sx_0)F+4s^2J.$$ The determinant of this linear change in $(F,J)$, over $\mathbb C[p,s,u]$, is $$4s^2x_0^2+(1+2sx_0)(1-2sx_0)=1.$$ Its inverse is <a id="con:linear-inverse"></a>$$\label{con:linear-inverse}
 F=4s^2L+(1+2sx_0)M,\qquad
 J=-(1-2sx_0)L+x_0^2M.$$ It follows that $p,s,u,L,M$ are algebraically independent and that $$P=B[L],\qquad B:=\mathbb C[p,s,u,M]\cong\mathbb C^{[4]}.$$ For the rest of the proof, regard $F,J,H$ as polynomials in $L$ over $B$, using [con:linear-inverse](#con:linear-inverse). Expansion only in powers of $p$ gives <a id="con:h-expansion"></a>$$\label{con:h-expansion}
 H(L)=L-pu+p^2Q(L)+p^4F(L)^3,
 \qquad Q(L)=2x_0F(L)^2-2sF(L)J(L)-J(L)^2.$$

At this point the transfer theorem of Edo–Vénéreau <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[EdoVenereau](#ref-EdoVenereau), Theorem 7] also applies, with coefficient ring $B$ and parameter $p^3$: every coefficient of $H(L)-L$ is divisible by $p$, hence nilpotent modulo $p^3B$. The following calculation gives a polynomial inverse in this instance.

*A polynomial root modulo $p^3$.* Define elements and a polynomial over $B$ by <a id="con:root"></a>$$\label{con:root}
 \begin{gathered}
 C=Q(0)=M^2(2x_0+6sx_0^2+4s^2x_0^3-x_0^4),\\
 Q_1(L)=\frac{Q(L)-C}{L}\in B[L],\qquad
 L_*=pu-p^2C\in B.
 \end{gathered}$$ Since $Q(L_*)-C=L_*Q_1(L_*)$, [con:h-expansion](#con:h-expansion) gives the exact identity <a id="con:root-divisibility"></a>$$\label{con:root-divisibility}
 H(L_*)=p^3\bigl((u-pC)Q_1(L_*)+pF(L_*)^3\bigr)\in p^3B.$$

*A polynomial representative for the fifth coordinate.* The quotient $(L-L_*)/p^3$ suggests the remaining coordinate after localization. The next identity provides a polynomial representative for it before $p$ is inverted. In $B[L,w]$, put <a id="con:e-polynomial"></a>$$\label{con:e-polynomial}
 h=u-pQ(L)-p^3F(L)^3-p^2w,
 \qquad e_0=-w-pF(L)^3-Q_1(L)h.$$ Then $H(L)+p^3w=L-ph$. Using $Q(L)-C=LQ_1(L)$, we obtain the polynomial identity <a id="con:e-certificate"></a>$$\label{con:e-certificate}
 p^3e_0-(L-L_*)
   =(p^2Q_1(L)-1)\bigl(H(L)+p^3w\bigr).$$ Thus the image $e$ of $e_0$ in $T$ satisfies <a id="con:e-relation"></a>$$\label{con:e-relation}
 p^3e=L-L_*.$$ In particular, $e$ belongs to the unlocalized ring $T$.

*Generation and algebraic independence.* Inverting $p$ in the presentation $T=B[L,w]/(H(L)+p^3w)$ allows us to eliminate $w$, giving the isomorphism <a id="con:t-localized"></a>$$\label{con:t-localized}
 T[p^{-1}]\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }B[p^{-1}][L],
 \qquad w\longmapsto-p^{-3}H(L),$$ which is the identity on $B$ and $L$. The composite $B\to T\to T[p^{-1}]$ is therefore the natural injection $B\hookrightarrow B[p^{-1}][L]$. This proves that the displayed copy of $B$ embeds in $T$. Moreover, [con:e-relation](#con:e-relation) identifies $e$ in this localization with $p^{-3}(L-L_*)$, an affine linear coordinate in the independent variable $L$. Consequently $e$ is transcendental over $B[p^{-1}]$, and hence over $B$.

It remains to prove that $B$ and $e$ generate $T$. By [con:e-relation](#con:e-relation), $L=L_*+p^3e$. The polynomial difference $H(L_*+p^3Z)-H(L_*)$ is divisible by $p^3$ in $B[Z]$. Together with [con:root-divisibility](#con:root-divisibility), this shows that $$W(Z):=-\frac{H(L_*+p^3Z)}{p^3}\in B[Z].$$ The defining relation of $T$ now gives $p^3(w-W(e))=0$. Since $T$ is a domain and $p\ne0$, it follows that $w=W(e)$. Thus $L,w\in B[e]$, which proves <a id="con:polynomial-cylinder"></a>$$\label{con:polynomial-cylinder}
 T=B[e]=\mathbb C[p,s,u,M,e]\cong\mathbb C^{[5]}.$$

For completeness, the five independent coordinates in $A[w]$ itself are the classes of $$\Phi^{-1}(p),\quad\Phi^{-1}(s),\quad\Phi^{-1}(u),\quad
 \Phi^{-1}(M),\quad\Phi^{-1}(e_0).$$ These are polynomials by the finite exponential formula. They are obtained by transporting the five generators in [con:polynomial-cylinder](#con:polynomial-cylinder) through the inverse of [con:cylinder-automorphism](#con:cylinder-automorphism), which proves the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:degeneration"></a>
### A smooth degeneration and its derivations

We retain the notation of [sec:construction](#sec:construction), using the same letters for elements of $P$ and their images in the domain $A=P/(H)$. The filtration constructed here will let us replace a locally nilpotent derivation of $A$ by a homogeneous one on a smooth algebra with a simpler equation.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### The valuation and its coefficients

<a id="deg:S-R"></a>
Consider the presented algebra $$\label{deg:S-R}
 S=\mathbb C[p,x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)-p^3u\bigr),
 \qquad
 R=S/pS=\mathbb C[x,y,z,u]/\bigl(xy-z(z+1)\bigr).$$ The identity $xy-z(z+1)=p^2(H+pu)$ defines a homomorphism $S\to A$.

<a id="stmt-deg-valuation"></a>
<div id="deg:valuation" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *The map $S\to A$ is injective, and $S[p^{-1}]=A[p^{-1}]$. The ring $R$ is a smooth domain. On the common fraction field of $S$ and $A$ there is a discrete valuation $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p$, normalized by $\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(p)=1$, coming from $S_{pS}$. For $b\in S$ and every integer $i\geq 0$, <a id="deg:global-divisibility"></a>$$\label{deg:global-divisibility}
 \mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(b)\geq i \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad b\in p^iS.$$ In particular, each nonzero $h\in S[p^{-1}]$ has a unique expression $h=p^m b$ with $m=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)\in\mathbb Z$ and $b\in S\setminus pS$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-deg-valuation"><summary>Proof of stmt-deg-valuation</summary>

*Proof.* After inverting $p$, the defining polynomial of $S$ is linear in $u$ with invertible leading coefficient. It is therefore irreducible in $\mathbb C[p,p^{-1},x,y,z,u]$. It is not divisible by $p$ in $\mathbb C[p,x,y,z,u]$, so it is irreducible there as well: any factor becoming a unit after localization would be a scalar times a power of $p$. The latter polynomial ring is a unique factorization domain, so $S$ is a domain and $p$ is nonzero in $S$. The inverse formulas $$s=y-x(x-u^3),\qquad
 F=\frac{x-s^2-u^3}{p^2},\qquad
 J=\frac{z-sx}{p^2}$$ show that $S\to A$ becomes an isomorphism after inverting $p$. Its kernel is consequently $p$-torsion, hence zero in the domain $S$. This also proves equality of the fraction fields.

The polynomial $xy-z(z+1)$ is primitive as a polynomial in $y$ over $\mathbb C[x,z,u]$, since $x$ and $z(z+1)$ are coprime. It is linear and irreducible over $\mathbb C(x,z,u)$, so $R$ is a domain. Its hypersurface is smooth by the Jacobian criterion <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks), Tags 00T6 and 00T7]: simultaneous vanishing of the partial derivatives $y$, $x$, and $-(2z+1)$ would give $x=y=0$, $z=-1/2$, which does not satisfy its equation.

Thus $pS$ is a nonzero prime ideal, and the noetherian local domain $T=S_{pS}$ has maximal ideal $pT$. To see directly that $T$ is a discrete valuation ring, divide a nonzero nonunit repeatedly by $p$. This process must end: otherwise the principal ideals generated by the successive quotients form an ascending chain, and stabilization would imply that $p$ is a unit, by cancellation in the domain $T$. Every nonzero element of $T$ is therefore a power of $p$ times a unit. This gives the stated valuation on $\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(T)$.

If $b\in S$ has positive order, then $b\in pT$, so $tb\in pS$ for some $t\in S\setminus pS$. Primality of $pS$ gives $b\in pS$. Dividing by $p$ and repeating proves [deg:global-divisibility](#deg:global-divisibility); its converse is immediate. Finally, write $h=p^{-N}b_0$ with $b_0\in S$, $N\geq 0$, and apply [deg:global-divisibility](#deg:global-divisibility) to $b_0$ to divide out its full order. The asserted expression follows, and its uniqueness follows from the valuation and cancellation. ◻

</details>

For $0\ne h\in A$ define its *valuation degree* by $\deg h=-\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_p(h)$, and put $\deg 0=-\infty$. In particular, $$\deg(hh')=\deg h+\deg h',\qquad
 \deg(h+h')\leq\max\{\deg h,\deg h'\}.$$ These formulas give an exhaustive, separated filtration indexed by all integers, $$\mathcal F_dA=\{h\in A:\deg h\leq d\},\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A=\bigoplus_{d\in\mathbb Z}\mathcal F_dA/\mathcal F_{d-1}A.$$ Write $[h]_d$ for the class of $h\in\mathcal F_dA$ in the $d$th summand, and $\mathop{\mathrm{in}}(h)=[h]_{\deg h}$ for a nonzero $h$. Give $R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$ the grading in which $R$ has degree zero and $\tau$ has degree $-1$. [deg:valuation](#deg:valuation) gives a graded injection <a id="deg:graded-injection"></a>$$\label{deg:graded-injection}
 \mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow R[\tau,\tau^{-1}],
 \qquad
 [h]_d\longmapsto \overline{p^dh}\,\tau^{-d}.$$ Indeed $p^dh\in S$ whenever $h\in\mathcal F_dA$, including when $d<0$, by the last assertion of [deg:valuation](#deg:valuation). Its residue modulo $pS$ vanishes exactly when $h\in\mathcal F_{d-1}A$. The displayed maps respect sums and products and are injective on each summand. This explains why their coefficients lie in $R$ itself.

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### The exact associated graded algebra

The initial forms of the five generators give a candidate for $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A$. The point requiring proof is that no cancellation among their leading terms produces an additional generator. We establish the presentation of the candidate first, then reduce the weight of any representative whose leading terms cancel.

In $R$ set <a id="deg:center"></a>$$\label{deg:center}
 s_R=y-x(x-u^3),\qquad
 f=x-s_R^2-u^3,\qquad
 g=z-s_Rx,\qquad I=(f,g).$$

<a id="stmt-prop-graded"></a>
<div id="prop:graded" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6**. *Under [deg:graded-injection](#deg:graded-injection), the associated graded algebra is the following specific subalgebra of $R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$: <a id="deg:G"></a>$$\label{deg:G}
 G:=\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A
   =R[\tau,I/\tau^2]
   =R[\tau,f/\tau^2,g/\tau^2].$$ It is a smooth domain. Give $P=\mathbb C[p,s,u,F,J]$ the presentation weights <a id="deg:presentation-weights"></a>$$\label{deg:presentation-weights}
 (\omega(p),\omega(s),\omega(u),\omega(F),\omega(J))
       =(-1,0,0,2,2).$$ Then every nonzero $h\in A$ has a polynomial representative whose maximum weight is exactly $\deg h$, and there is a graded isomorphism <a id="deg:graded-presentation"></a>$$\label{deg:graded-presentation}
 G\cong P/(H_{\mathrm{top}}),\qquad
 H_{\mathrm{top}}=x^2F-(1+2sx)J-p^2J^2,
 \quad x=s^2+u^3+p^2F.$$ The images of $p,s,u,F,J$ in this isomorphism are respectively $\tau,s_R,u,f/\tau^2,g/\tau^2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-graded"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-graded</summary>

*Proof.* The residues of $s$ and $u$ in $R$ are the nonzero elements $s_R$ and $u$. Also $f$ and $g$ are nonzero: specializing $x=z=0$ sends $f$ to $-y^2-u^3$, and specializing $x=0$, $z=-1$ sends $g$ to $-1$. The identities $p^2F=x-s^2-u^3$ and $p^2J=z-sx$ therefore give <a id="deg:initial-generators"></a>$$\label{deg:initial-generators}
 \begin{array}{c|ccccc}
 h&p&s&u&F&J\\ \hline
 \deg h&-1&0&0&2&2\\
 \mathop{\mathrm{in}}(h)&\tau&s_R&u&f/\tau^2&g/\tau^2.
 \end{array}$$ Here $s_R$ is nonzero because its specialization at $x=z=0$ is $y$.

Let $\theta:P\to R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$ substitute the five initial forms in [deg:initial-generators](#deg:initial-generators). Its image contains the specified copy of $R$: the generators of that copy are recovered by <a id="deg:recover-R"></a>$$\label{deg:recover-R}
 \begin{split}
 x&=s_R^2+u^3+\tau^2(f/\tau^2),\\
 y&=s_R+x(x-u^3),\\
 z&=s_Rx+\tau^2(g/\tau^2).
 \end{split}$$ Consequently $\theta(P)$ is exactly the subalgebra on the right of [deg:G](#deg:G). In particular this is an identification over the given coefficient ring $R$.

For the presentation weights, $x$ has weight zero and $H_{\mathrm{top}}$ is homogeneous of weight two, with <a id="deg:top-relation"></a>$$\label{deg:top-relation}
 H=H_{\mathrm{top}}-pu,
 \qquad xy-z(z+1)=p^2H_{\mathrm{top}}.$$ After inverting $p$, the coordinate change from $(s,u,F,J)$ to $(x,y,z,u)$ and its inverse in [deg:valuation](#deg:valuation) identify $\theta$ with the quotient map $$\mathbb C[p,p^{-1},x,y,z,u]\longrightarrow R[\tau,\tau^{-1}],
 \qquad p\longmapsto\tau.$$ Its kernel is $(xy-z(z+1))$, or equivalently $(H_{\mathrm{top}})$ in the original localized coordinates. This is a prime ideal. Moreover $p$ does not divide $H_{\mathrm{top}}$, since its reduction modulo $p$ is $$x_0^2F-(1+2sx_0)J,\qquad x_0=s^2+u^3,$$ which is nonzero. The same unique factorization argument as in [deg:valuation](#deg:valuation) shows that $H_{\mathrm{top}}$ is irreducible, hence prime, in $P$. Its principal ideal is thus saturated with respect to $p$: if $p^NQ\in(H_{\mathrm{top}})$ then $Q\in(H_{\mathrm{top}})$. Contracting the localized kernel yields <a id="deg:kernel"></a>$$\label{deg:kernel}
 \ker\theta=(H_{\mathrm{top}}).$$

It remains to show that these initial forms generate all of $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A$. For a nonzero polynomial $Q\in P$, let $\omega(Q)$ be its maximum monomial weight. If $Q$ represents a nonzero $h\in A$, then $\deg h\leq\omega(Q)$ by [deg:initial-generators](#deg:initial-generators) and the valuation inequalities. Suppose the inequality is strict, and write $M=\omega(Q)$. The weight-$M$ part $Q_M$ has $\theta(Q_M)=0$, since the degree-$M$ class of $h$ is zero. By [deg:kernel](#deg:kernel), $$Q_M=H_{\mathrm{top}}C,$$ where $C$ may be taken homogeneous of weight $M-2$. To justify the homogeneity, decompose any quotient $C$ into its finitely many homogeneous parts; multiplication by the nonzero homogeneous $H_{\mathrm{top}}$ cannot cancel contributions of different weights in the domain $P$. The polynomial $$Q-HC=Q-Q_M+puC$$ represents the same element $h$ and has maximum weight strictly less than $M$, because $puC$ has weight $M-3$. Repeating this operation terminates: the maximum weights are strictly decreasing integers bounded below by the fixed finite number $\deg h$. Thus it ends with a representative of weight exactly $\deg h$. No lower bound on the weights of all monomials in $P$ is needed.

The initial form of $h$ is now the image under $\theta$ of the top part of such an exact representative. This proves $\mathop{\mathrm{gr}}A=\theta(P)$ and, together with [deg:kernel](#deg:kernel), proves both [deg:G](#deg:G) and [deg:graded-presentation](#deg:graded-presentation).

Finally, $G$ is a domain by its embedding in $R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$. On the open set where $p$, or equivalently $\tau$, is invertible, the presentation gives the smooth algebra $R[\tau,\tau^{-1}]$. On $p=0$, the partial derivatives of $H_{\mathrm{top}}$ with respect to $F$ and $J$ reduce to $x_0^2$ and $-(1+2sx_0)$. They cannot vanish simultaneously, as $$(1-2sx_0)(1+2sx_0)+4s^2x_0^2=1.$$ The Jacobian criterion proves smoothness also at every point of $p=0$, and hence on all of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}G$. ◻

</details>

The weights in [deg:presentation-weights](#deg:presentation-weights) compute the valuation degree on $G$. This degree will remain distinct from the fiber weight and the auxiliary degree introduced later.

The description $G=R[\tau,I/\tau^2]$ is an affine modification in the sense of Kaliman–Zaidenberg <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[KalimanZaidenberg](#ref-KalimanZaidenberg), Definition 1.1]: the base ring is $R[\tau]$, the ideal is $(\tau^2,I)$, and the element inverted in the construction is $\tau^2$. Here the exact graded presentation, including its coefficient ring, is what permits the later bundle pullback.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Inducing locally nilpotent derivations

We now transfer a derivation of $A$ to its associated graded algebra. The exact representative statement in [prop:graded](#prop:graded) is the needed input: a bound on the five generators then bounds the degree shift on every element of $A$. This is the filtered-to-homogeneous derivation method developed by Kaliman–Makar-Limanov <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[KalimanMakarLimanov](#ref-KalimanMakarLimanov), Sections 3–5]. We give the argument for the present integer-indexed filtration, including the possibility of negative degrees.

A $\mathbb C$-derivation of a $\mathbb C$-algebra $C$ is a $\mathbb C$-linear map $D:C\to C$ satisfying $D(hh')=hD(h')+h'D(h)$. It is *locally nilpotent* if for each $h\in C$ there is an integer $N\geq 1$ such that $D^Nh=0$; all subsequent iterates then vanish as well. We abbreviate this by LND. On a graded algebra a derivation is *homogeneous of degree shift $j$* if it sends the component of degree $d$ to that of degree $d+j$ for every $d\in\mathbb Z$.

<a id="stmt-lem-induced-lnd"></a>
<div id="lem:induced-lnd" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7**. *Every nonzero LND $D$ of $A$ induces a nonzero homogeneous LND $D_0$ of $G$. If $0\ne h\in\ker D$, then $\mathop{\mathrm{in}}(h)\in\ker D_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-induced-lnd"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-induced-lnd</summary>

*Proof.* Let $(h_1,\ldots,h_5)=(p,s,u,F,J)$ be the generators of $A$ and set <a id="deg:shift"></a>$$\label{deg:shift}
 j=\max_{Dh_i\ne0}\bigl(\deg(Dh_i)-\deg h_i\bigr).$$ The maximum is over a finite nonempty set, since $D$ is nonzero. We claim that <a id="deg:shift-bound"></a>$$\label{deg:shift-bound}
 \deg(Dh)\leq\deg h+j\qquad(0\ne h\in A).$$ By [prop:graded](#prop:graded), choose a representative of $h$ of maximum weight exactly $\deg h$. Applying the product rule to each monomial, every nonzero summand in its derivative has degree at most the monomial’s weight plus $j$: differentiating a factor $h_i$ changes its degree by at most $j$. Summing these bounds gives [deg:shift-bound](#deg:shift-bound).

It follows that $D(\mathcal F_dA)\subseteq\mathcal F_{d+j}A$, so <a id="deg:induced-map"></a>$$\label{deg:induced-map}
 D_0([h]_d)=[Dh]_{d+j}$$ defines a homogeneous derivation of degree shift $j$ on $G$. It is well defined because $D(\mathcal F_{d-1}A)$ lies in $\mathcal F_{d+j-1}A$, and the product rule descends to the graded algebra. For a generator attaining the maximum in [deg:shift](#deg:shift), the right side of [deg:induced-map](#deg:induced-map) applied to its initial form is nonzero. Thus $D_0\ne0$.

For every $h\in\mathcal F_dA$ and $m\geq 0$, induction gives the identity in the indicated graded component <a id="deg:iterate"></a>$$\label{deg:iterate}
 D_0^m([h]_d)=[D^mh]_{d+mj}.$$ It includes the case when an intermediate class vanishes: the shift bound ensures that a drop below the indicated degree persists in all later iterates. Local nilpotence of $D$ therefore kills each homogeneous class after finitely many iterates of $D_0$. Every element of $G$ is a finite sum of homogeneous classes, so a maximum of their nilpotence bounds kills that element. This proves local nilpotence on all of $G$, without a sign restriction on $j$. Finally, if $Dh=0$, [deg:induced-map](#deg:induced-map) gives $D_0\mathop{\mathrm{in}}(h)=0$. ◻

</details>

The positive-degree-invariant obstruction is inspired by Gupta’s Derksen-invariant strategy; see <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[GuptaThreefold](#ref-GuptaThreefold), Lemma 2.4 and the proof of Proposition 3.6]. Here it is expressed directly through polynomial coordinates. The characteristic-zero construction and the subsequent bundle and rigidity arguments are proved in this paper; Gupta’s positive-characteristic theorem is not an input to them.

<a id="stmt-prop-positive-invariant"></a>
<div id="prop:positive-invariant" class="proposition">

**Proposition 8**. *If $A\cong\mathbb C^{[4]}$, then $G$ admits a nonzero homogeneous LND with a nonzero homogeneous invariant of strictly positive valuation degree.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-positive-invariant"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-positive-invariant</summary>

*Proof.* Under the hypothesis choose polynomial coordinates $q_1,q_2,q_3,q_4$ of $A$. The subset $\mathcal F_0A$ is a $\mathbb C$-subalgebra. Since $F$ has degree two by [deg:initial-generators](#deg:initial-generators), this subalgebra is proper. Therefore at least one coordinate $q_r$ has positive degree; otherwise the coordinates would generate $A$ inside $\mathcal F_0A$. Choose $i\ne r$. The coordinate partial derivative $D=\partial/\partial q_i$ is a nonzero LND of $A$ and satisfies $Dq_r=0$. By [lem:induced-lnd](#lem:induced-lnd), its induced derivation $D_0$ on $G$ is nonzero and homogeneous, and it kills the nonzero homogeneous element $\mathop{\mathrm{in}}(q_r)$ of degree $\deg q_r>0$. ◻

</details>

Neither [lem:induced-lnd](#lem:induced-lnd) nor [prop:positive-invariant](#prop:positive-invariant) asserts that $D_0$ preserves the subalgebra $R\subset G$. Coefficient preservation can fail even for a homogeneous LND on $G$. The derivation $\Delta$ in [con:derivation](#con:derivation) has presentation-weight shift $-2$ and satisfies $\Delta(H_{\mathrm{top}})=\Delta(H+pu)=p^3-p^3=0$. It therefore descends through [deg:graded-presentation](#deg:graded-presentation) to an LND on $G$ sending $u\in R$ to $-\tau^2\notin R$. This example concerns actions on $G$: $\Delta$ does not descend to $A$, since $\Delta H=p^3$. The next section lifts the action on $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}G$ directly, without assuming $D_0(R)\subset R$.

<a id="sec:bundle"></a>
### Principalization and lifted additive actions

The ideal defining $G$ becomes principal after pullback to a line bundle with its zero section removed. We construct that bundle and then show that every homogeneous additive action on $G$ lifts to the pullback.
